1	# Deploy KhelSutra Studio for alpha — a shareable page, no SSH
2	
3	> **Status:** `READY` (owner executes the cloud steps) · **Topology:** split — SPA on Cloudflare Pages + engine on an `api.*` cloudflared tunnel, Cloudflare Access over both (the engine's `HOSTING_PLAN.md` §2 alpha plan). · **Cost:** ~$0/mo standing (Pages + Tunnel + Access are free ≤50 users) + Gemini per-run (~$0.05/match).
4	
5	This turns the local-only flow (run engine + SPA on your box, drive it yourself) into a **page you can share with alpha testers** — they open `app.khelsutra.guru`, sign in (email allowlist), point at a video, and get a reel. No SSH, no home IP exposed, no inbound ports.
6	
7	## 0. The shape
8	
9	```
10	 tester browser
11	    │ https
12	    ▼
13	 Cloudflare edge (free): DNS · TLS · Access (email-OTP allowlist) in front of BOTH hostnames
14	    ├── app.khelsutra.guru  → Cloudflare Pages         (the web/ SPA; built with VITE_API_BASE)
15	    └── api.khelsutra.guru  → cloudflared Tunnel (outbound-only) ─► 127.0.0.1:8000 on YOUR box
16	                                                                       │  FastAPI engine
17	                                                                       │  (Gemini segmenter; GPU optional)
18	                                                                       ▼  async jobs, SQLite, output/ on disk
19	```
20	
21	- **No inbound ports.** `cloudflared` dials out; the engine stays bound to `127.0.0.1:8000`. The box's home/public IP is never exposed.
22	- **Auth at the edge.** Cloudflare Access gates both hostnames; the engine *also* verifies the `Cf-Access` JWT in-app (defence in depth — `security.edge_auth_enabled`), so a direct hit to the tunnel can't spoof identity.
23	- **Cross-origin, on purpose.** `app.` (SPA) and `api.` (engine) are different origins → the SPA is built with `VITE_API_BASE=https://api.khelsutra.guru/api` (PR #64) and the engine locks CORS to the SPA origin via `RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` (no engine code change — it's already env-driven).
24	
25	> **Alternative (single-origin):** if you'd rather run **one box** that serves the SPA *and* the API behind one origin (Caddy reverse-proxy, no Pages, no cross-origin), see `LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` D1. This runbook ships the **split** topology you chose (stable Pages-hosted page + a thin API tunnel).
26	
27	---
28	
29	## 1. Prerequisites
30	
31	- A **box** to run the engine: the CPU droplet `168.144.126.176` (Gemini-only — no GPU; fine for alpha) **or** your Windows+WSL GPU box (real WASB). `ffmpeg` + Python 3.8+ installed; the `badminton-highlight-indexer` engine checked out.
32	- A **Cloudflare account** with the `khelsutra.guru` zone (you already run the marketing site there).
33	- `cloudflared` installed on the box (`https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/connections/connect-networks/downloads/`).
34	- A **fresh** `GEMINI_API_KEY` (rotate the chat-shared one — it uploads downsized chunks to Google; this is a paid + cloud action).
35	
36	---
37	
38	## 2. Engine (the `api.` side)
39	
40	### 2.1 Install (with the auth extra)
41	```bash
42	cd badminton-highlight-indexer
43	pip install -e .[auth]          # PyJWT[crypto] — REQUIRED when edge auth is on, else every request 500s
44	```
45	
46	### 2.2 The safe-exposed config
47	Create / edit `config.json` on the box:
48	```jsonc
49	{
50	  "security": {
51	    "edge_auth_enabled": true,                       // turn the CF-Access JWT verify ON
52	    "allowed_video_root": "/srv/khelsutra/incoming",  // the path-jail — REQUIRED when exposed
53	    "upload_dir": "/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads",  // MUST sit UNDER allowed_video_root (see §7)
54	    "cf_team_domain": "https://<your-team>.cloudflareaccess.com",
55	    "cf_access_aud": "<the CF Access application AUD>"  // filled in §4
56	  }
57	}
58	```
59	And export the env (a `.env` or your shell):
60	```bash
61	export GEMINI_API_KEY="<your-rotated-key>"
62	export RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS="https://app.khelsutra.guru"  ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

90	def _exposure_checks(config: Any, env: Mapping[str, str]) -> list[StartupCheck]:
91	    """Exposure-safety posture for an *exposed* HTTP server. Gated on ``security.edge_auth_enabled``
92	    (default False → ``[]`` = strict no-op, today's behaviour). When edge auth is ON — the "I am

### 40. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.841Z)

Decisive — and it overturns part of the skeptic's suggested fix. Reading the actual `origin/master` code:

- `TrustedHostMiddleware` is added **unconditionally** at import ([main.py:108-112](backend/main.py)) with `allowed_hosts=_allowed_hosts_from_env()`.
- `_allowed_hosts_from_env()` ([main.py:88-102](backend/main.py)) reads **only** `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS`; default `["localhost","127.0.0.1","testserver"]`. It is **completely decoupled** from the bind host and from `edge_auth`.
- `_resolve_bind_host()` ([main.py:115-128](backend/main.py)) is a *separate* function that only sets the socket bind.

**So the skeptic's "or set `RALLY_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1`" is NOT a valid fix for the 400.** That changes only the bind; cloudflared still forwards `Host: api.khelsutra.guru`, still not in the loopback-only allowlist → **still 400**. The mandatory, load-bearing fix is **`RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru"`** — independent of bind. This is exactly the kind of subtle-but-wrong fix that independent verification catches.

Now let me empirically prove both blockers hermetically ($0, local — no droplet/GPU needed for this). First probe the Python env, then repro.

from starlette.applications import Starlette
from starlette.responses import PlainTextResponse
from starlette.routing import Route
from starlette.middleware.trustedhost import TrustedHostMiddleware
from starlette.testclient import TestClient

# These are the engine's values, read verbatim from origin/master backend/main.py:
DEFAULT_ALLOWLIST = ["localhost", "127.0.0.1", "testserver"]   # main.py:102  (RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS UNSET — what the doc prescribes)
FIXED_ALLOWLIST   = ["api.khelsutra.guru"]                     # main.py:97-98 (RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru" — the fix)

def health(req): return PlainTextResponse("ok")
def app_with(allow):
    a = Starlette(routes=[Route("/api/health", health)])
    a.add_middleware(TrustedHostMiddleware, allowed_hosts=allow, www_redirect=False)  # main.py:108-112 verbatim
    return a

c1 = TestClient(app_with(DEFAULT_ALLOWLIST), raise_server_exceptions=False)
c2 = TestClient(app_with(FIXED_ALLOWLIST), raise_server_exceptions=False)
print("DOC AS WRITTEN (no RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS):")

print("   loopback Host=127.0.0.1                      ->", c1.get("/api/health", headers={"Host":"127.0.0.1"}).status_code)
print("WITH THE FIX (RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru):")
print("   cloudflared forwards Host=api.khelsutra.guru ->", c2.get("/api/health", headers={"Host":"api.khelsutra.guru"}).status_code, " <- FIXED")
PY

38	## 2. Engine (the `api.` side)
39	
40	### 2.1 Install (with the auth extra)
41	```bash
42	cd badminton-highlight-indexer
43	pip install -e .[auth]          # PyJWT[crypto] — REQUIRED when edge auth is on, else every authenticated request is rejected 401
44	```
45	
46	### 2.2 The safe-exposed config
47	Create / edit `config.json` on the box. **Where it must live:** the engine reads `$RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json` if `RALLY_APP_HOME` is set, else `./config.json` relative to *the directory you launch the server from* — **not** necessarily the repo root. If the engine reads a different (or absent) file it silently falls back to all-defaults, which means **edge auth is OFF**. To make writer and reader agree by construction, write it with `python -m backend.main --setup` (it targets the exact path the server reads) and edit *that* file:
48	```jsonc
49	{
50	  "security": {
51	    "edge_auth_enabled": true,                       // turn the CF-Access JWT verify ON
52	    "allowed_video_root": "/srv/khelsutra/incoming",  // the path-jail — REQUIRED when exposed
53	    "upload_dir": "/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads",  // MUST sit UNDER allowed_video_root (see §7)
54	    "cf_team_domain": "https://<your-team>.cloudflareaccess.com",
55	    "cf_access_aud": "<the CF Access application AUD>"  // filled in §4
56	  }
57	}
58	```
59	And export the env (a `.env` or your shell):
60	```bash
61	export GEMINI_API_KEY="<your-rotated-key>"
62	export RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS="https://app.khelsutra.guru"   # lock CORS to the SPA origin
63	export RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru"                # REQUIRED — see ⚠ below; without it EVERY tunneled request 400s
64	export RALLY_BIND_HOST="127.0.0.1"                             # keep the loopback bind (cloudflared dials 127.0.0.1:8000)
65	```
66	
67	> **⚠ Two env vars you MUST set, or the tunnel is dead-on-arrival.** With `edge_auth_enabled: true`, the engine (a) flips its bind to `0.0.0.0` and (b) keeps the DNS-rebinding **Host-header guard** at its loopback-only default — so it boots "healthy" but then **`400`s every request `cloudflared` forwards with `Host: api.khelsutra.guru`** (including the §6 `/api/health` check). The boot only *warns* about this (`EXPOSED_HOST_ALLOWLIST_LOOPBACK`), it does not stop. Set **`RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru"`** (the load-bearing fix — the Host allowlist is independent of the bind) and **`RALLY_BIND_HOST="127.0.0.1"`** (so the bind stays loopback as §0/§6 claim; `cloudflared` dials loopback anyway). *Verified against engine `origin/master`: with the allowlist unset, `GET /api/health` with `Host: api.khelsutra.guru` → `400`; with it set → `200`.*
68	
69	> **The engine refuses to start unsafe.** The boot posture (engine PR #294) **fails fast** if `edge_auth_enabled` is on but the `allowed_video_root` jail or `cf_team_domain`/`cf_access_aud` is missing, and **warns** if `PyJWT` isn't installed. So a half-configured exposure can't silently come up — fix what it prints and restart.
70	
71	### 2.3 Run
72	```bash
73	python -m backend.main --server     # binds 127.0.0.1:8000 — kept loopback by RALLY_BIND_HOST (§2.2); with edge_auth ON and that var unset it would bind 0.0.0.0 instead
74	```
75	Keep it alive with a process manager (`systemd` on the droplet, mirroring `site/scripts/provision-vm.sh`'s unit; or `pm2`/`nssm` on Windows).
76	
77	---
78	
79	## 3. The SPA on Cloudflare Pages (the `app.` side)
80	
81	The SPA ships as a **static Pages site**, git-connected like the marketing site (auto-deploy on merge to `master`).
82

116	---
117	
118	## 6. Verify (the posture test + the CUJ)
119	
120	**Exposure posture (must hold — this is what makes it safe to share):**
121	- From OUTSIDE the box: `curl --max-time 5 http://<box-public-ip>:8000/api/health` → **refused / timeout** (the engine is `127.0.0.1`-bound; no inbound port). ✔ The box is not directly reachable.
122	- `curl -i https://api.khelsutra.guru/api/health` **without** a CF session → a **302 to the Access login** (not a 200). ✔ The gate is in front.
123	- Open `https://app.khelsutra.guru` in a browser → CF Access login → after sign-in, the SPA loads and `/api/health` returns 200 (same session cookie). ✔
124	
125	**The shareable CUJ (what a tester does):** open `app.khelsutra.guru` → sign in → **upload a video from their device** (the SPA chunked-upload lands it in `upload_dir`, inside the jail) → **Process** → watch progress → pick rallies → **Build** → **download the reel**. All from the page. *(Pasting a `gs://…` URI does NOT work on this box — see §7: the `allowed_video_root` jail rejects every non-local URI. Browser upload is the alpha path.)*
126	
127	---
128	
129	## 7. Honest caveats / deferred
130	
131	- **`upload_dir` must sit UNDER `allowed_video_root`.** If you enable browser upload, an upload landing outside the jail is rejected by `/api/process` (a functional break, not a hole). Keep `upload_dir` inside the jail (§2.2).
132	- **`gs://` / `s3://` / `gdrive://` URIs are REJECTED on the exposed box.** Setting `allowed_video_root` (required to expose, §2.2) path-jails `/api/process` to *local* paths under that root; the engine resolves a `gs://…` URI to a local path that escapes the jail → `400` (pinned by engine `tests/test_api_endpoints.py::test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri`). Cloud-URI ingest works **only** when `allowed_video_root` is unset (the local single-user posture — the opposite of this runbook). For alpha, the ingest path is **browser upload** (it lands inside the jail). *(Scheme-exempting recognized buckets from the jail would be an engine change, not a config flip.)*
133	- **You MUST set `edge_auth_enabled=true` to expose.** The boot posture (and the in-app JWT verify) only engage then. Do not put the engine behind the tunnel with edge auth off.
134	- **Cross-origin reel download:** the reel streams from `api.` with `Content-Disposition: attachment`, so it downloads even cross-origin — but verify the filename in your browser during the CUJ (cross-origin `<a download>` filename hints are ignored; the header is the authority).
135	- **Upload size:** Cloudflare's free proxy caps request bodies at **100 MB** → large matches upload as ~95 MB chunks (the SPA chunked-upload path honours the engine's `max_part_mb`, default **95**; lower `security.max_part_mb` to shrink them). `gs://` bucket pre-staging is **not** an option on this box (see the jail caveat above).
136	- **Box offline = engine offline.** The Pages SPA stays up and shows a friendly "engine offline" banner (`/api/health` ping); processing resumes when the box is back. Beta removes this with a queue (`HOSTING_PLAN.md` §3).
137	- **Gemini = paid + uploads footage to Google.** Keep the key rotated; use it only for clips you're OK sending.
138	
139	---

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Open PR1
command: WEB=/c/Users/avidu/Projects/khelsutra
gh pr create --repo avidullu/khelsutra --base master --head fix/deploy-alpha-runbook-correctness \
  --title "docs(deploy): fix two would-break-the-deploy gaps in the alpha runbook (RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS + gs:// jail)" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Why
`deploy/DEPLOY_ALPHA.md` is the owner-executed alpha deploy runbook (split: CF Pages + `api.*` cloudflared tunnel + CF Access). I adversarially verified every concrete command/config/safety-claim against engine **`origin/master`** and **reproduced the failures hermetically** (in-process, $0 — no droplet/GPU). Two gaps would have the owner follow the doc to the letter and get **nothing working**.

## Blocker 1 — `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` was never set (the CUJ-killer)
With `edge_auth_enabled: true`, the engine (a) flips its bind to `0.0.0.0` and (b) keeps the DNS-rebinding **Host-header guard** (`TrustedHostMiddleware`) at its **loopback-only default** `["localhost","127.0.0.1","testserver"]`. The allowlist is **decoupled from the bind** (`backend/main.py:88-112` on `origin/master`), so `cloudflared` forwarding `Host: api.khelsutra.guru` gets **`400`'d on every request** — including `/api/health` in the §6 verify step. The engine only emits a non-fatal boot WARN (`EXPOSED_HOST_ALLOWLIST_LOOPBACK`), so it comes up "healthy" then 400s everything.

**Repro on `origin/master` (real Starlette `TrustedHostMiddleware`, exact engine allowlist):**
```
RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS unset:  GET /api/health Host=api.khelsutra.guru -> 400
RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru:  same request -> 200
```
**Fix:** added `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru"` (load-bearing) **+** `RALLY_BIND_HOST="127.0.0.1"` to §2.2. Note: `RALLY_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1` alone does **not** fix the 400 (it only changes the bind); the loopback pin is there so the bind stays loopback as §0/§6 already claim — `cloudflared` dials loopback regardless.

## Blocker 2 — `gs://` ingest is impossible on the exposed box
`allowed_video_root` (required to expose, §2.2) path-jails `/api/process` to **local** paths. The engine resolves a `gs://…` URI to a local path that escapes the jail → **`400`**. Pinned by engine `tests/test_api_endpoints.py::test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri`; reproduced here for `gs://`, `s3://`, `gdrive://`, and `../escape` (all rejected; a local file under the jail passes). The §6 CUJ ("paste a `gs://…` video") and §7 ("ingest the `gs://` URI (already supported)") were **mutually exclusive** with the required `allowed_video_root`.

**Fix:** dropped the `gs://` promise from §6/§7; the alpha ingest path is **browser upload** into `upload_dir` (inside the jail). True `gs://`-on-exposed would need an engine change (scheme-exempt before the jail), noted as such.

## Minor accuracy fixes
- §2.1: "every request 500s" → **401** (missing `.[auth]` fails *closed*, not 500 — `auth.py` raises `EdgeAuthError` → `main.py` maps to 401).
- §2.2: where `config.json` must live — `$RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json` else `./config.json` relative to the server's CWD; recommend `python -m backend.main --setup` so writer/reader agree (a misplaced file silently runs on defaults = **auth OFF**).
- §2.3: bind comment reflects the `RALLY_BIND_HOST` pin.
- §7: chunk size `≤90 MB` → **~95 MB** (engine `max_part_mb`, default 95; under CF's 100 MB cap).

## Scope / safety
Doc-only; **no engine or SPA code change**. The deploy works with just these doc fixes. The optional **engine-side** improvement (escalate the Host-allowlist WARN to a fail-fast like the other exposure checks, or scheme-exempt recognized buckets from the jail) belongs to the serving session that owns `backend/config/startup.py` — flagged to them, not done here.

## What's still owner-verify (needs the live Cloudflare account)
CF Access AUD/team-domain wiring, the tunnel/DNS, the Pages build, the free-tier 100 MB / ≤50-user limits, and that no inbound port 8000 is opened on the box firewall. These can't be checked locally.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -3
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## The two gaps that would have killed your afternoon

I verified `DEPLOY_ALPHA.md` against the **real deploy code** (engine `origin/master`, *not* this checkout's stale serving branch) with an 11-agent workflow + skeptic, then **reproduced both failures hermetically**:

1. **`RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` was missing.** With `edge_auth` on, the engine binds `0.0.0.0` but keeps its Host-header guard at the loopback-only default — so it boots "healthy" then **`400`s every request `cloudflared` forwards** (only a boot WARN). Repro: `/api/health` with `Host: api.khelsutra.guru` → **400 unset, 200 set**. I also **corrected the verifier's own suggested fix** — `RALLY_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1` does *not* fix it (the allowlist is decoupled from the bind); `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS="api.khelsutra.guru"` is the load-bearing one. #78 adds both.
2. **`gs://` ingest is impossible on the exposed box.** The required `allowed_video_root` jail rejects `gs://`/`s3://`/`gdrive://` (repro'd 400; pinned by `test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri`). The §6 CUJ promised it — dropped; the alpha path is **browser upload**.

**I did not spin up the droplet or GPU VM** — and didn't need to. These proofs are deterministic and hermetic at **$0**; the GPU is irrelevant to a CPU+Gemini alpha. The only genuinely un-local bits (real CF Access AUD, tunnel/DNS, Pages build, the box firewall) require *your* Cloudflare account, so I've listed those as owner-verify rather than burn spend guessing. If you want a live boot+curl smoke on the droplet against the corrected config, I can do it — but it needs your SSH access + a rotated key, and the hermetic repro already settles correctness.

## What's still yours (genuinely owner-gated infra)
Cloudflare Pages + Access setup, the `cloudflared` tunnel, **rotating `GEMINI_API_KEY`**, and confirming no inbound port 8000 on the box. The corrected runbook now walks all of it, including the `config.json` location trap (`--setup`) and the chunk-size reality.

## One thing to route to the serving session
The engine's Host-allowlist misconfig is only a boot **WARN**, while the *other* exposure misconfigs fail-fast — it's the one that 100%-breaks a deploy yet doesn't stop boot. Worth escalating WARN→ERROR in `backend/config/startup.py`, but that's the live `serving/exposure-safety-preflight` session's code, so I flagged it (in #78's description + the handoff) rather than touch their branch. #78 unblocks you regardless.

### 135. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.846Z)

Here's where we land on each point.

## 2. The $25 plan — **no, you don't need it.** Don't buy it.
Every piece of this runs on **free tiers**:

| Component | Plan needed | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| **Cloudflare Pages** (the SPA) | Free (unlimited sites/requests, 500 builds/mo) | $0 |
| **Cloudflare Tunnel** (`cloudflared`, the `api.` side) | Free | $0 |
| **Cloudflare Access** (the email gate) | Free — **up to 50 users** | $0 |
| `khelsutra.guru` DNS zone | you already have it | $0 |

So standing Cloudflare cost is **$0**; your only spend is Gemini per-run + the droplet you already pay for. If you saw "$25/mo," that's a **Pro zone** or **Workers Paid** upsell — **neither is required here.** The one thing to expect: when you first enable **Zero Trust**, Cloudflare may ask you to *add a payment method* and "subscribe to the **Free** plan" — that's a $0 plan that just wants a card on file; you won't be charged under 50 users. Pick **Free**, not a paid tier.

## 3. Hostnames — confirmed, and you're right that they're created *during* setup
Nothing pre-exists; they get created as you go and "baked in" in two spots:
- **`api.khelsutra.guru`** → created when you run `cloudflared tunnel route dns` (it writes the CNAME). Baked into the engine env (`RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, CORS origin) + the SPA's `VITE_API_BASE`.
- **`app.khelsutra.guru`** → created when you add it as a **custom domain** on the Pages project. Baked into the Access app + CORS.

I'll set all those references to these two names.

## Inputs locked
✅ Droplet / Linux / `systemd`  ·  ✅ default jail paths `/srv/khelsutra/incoming` + `…/uploads`  ·  ✅ allowlist `avi.dullu@gmail.com`. On the path-portability point: nothing hardcodes the Linux path — `allowed_video_root`/`upload_dir` are just config strings, so a Windows+WSL user later sets e.g. `C:\khelsutra\incoming\…` with zero code change. Good instinct to keep that open.

## The one input still missing: your **Zero Trust team name**
The `<team>` in `https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com` → it becomes `security.cf_team_domain` in the engine config. If Zero Trust is already set up, it's at **Zero Trust → Settings → Custom Pages / team domain**. If not set up yet, you **choose** it on first enable — just tell me what you'll pick (or I'll leave a single `<team>` placeholder). (The other CF-generated value, the Access **AUD**, only exists *after* you create the Access app — that one's always a paste-back step.)

## One snag on "use the droplet for testing"
I probed it: the IP is in my `known_hosts` (something connected before), but **`root@168.144.126.176` rejects my key** (`Permission denied (publickey)`), and `cloudflared`/python status came back blocked. So I **can't currently drive the droplet**. To test/verify there, pick one:

- **you run the commands**, paste the output, and I verify each step.

(I'd rather we *run* the engine-side on the droplet before I commit any provisioning script as "tested" — that's the exact "don't ship unverified deploy assets" lesson from #78.)

| File | What you do with it |

| `config.json.example` | → `/srv/khelsutra/config.json` (JSON-validated; just fill the **AUD** after creating the Access app) |
| `engine.env.example` | → `/etc/khelsutra/engine.env` (fill your **rotated GEMINI key**; the two critical `RALLY_*` vars are pre-set) |

description: Live engine verification on the droplet (boot posture + Host-guard fix)
command: KEY=~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet

cat > /tmp/verify_engine.sh <<'SCRIPT'
#!/bin/bash
PY=/opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/python
REPO=/opt/khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer
cd "$REPO"
hr(){ echo "----------------------------------------"; }
run_server(){ rm -f /tmp/eng.log; env $1 $PY -m backend.main --server >/tmp/eng.log 2>&1 & SVPID=$!
  for i in $(seq 1 40); do curl -s -o /dev/null --max-time 1 http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/health 2>/dev/null && return 0
    kill -0 $SVPID 2>/dev/null || return 1; sleep 0.5; done; return 2; }
stop_server(){ kill $SVPID 2>/dev/null; wait $SVPID 2>/dev/null; }
code(){ curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" --max-time 3 -H "Host: $1" http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/health; }

echo "### 0. backend.main imports in the venv"; $PY -c "import backend.main as m; print('   import OK; app =', m.app.title)"

hr; echo "### 1. boot-posture FAIL-FAST: edge_auth=true but NO aud -> must refuse to start (exit 1)"
mkdir -p /tmp/cfg_noaud; printf '%s' '{"security":{"edge_auth_enabled":true,"allowed_video_root":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming","upload_dir":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads","cf_team_domain":"https://khelsutra.cloudflareaccess.com","cf_access_aud":""}}' > /tmp/cfg_noaud/config.json
env RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/cfg_noaud $PY -m backend.main --server >/tmp/ff.log 2>&1; echo "   exit code: $? (expect 1)"; grep -ioE "refusing to start|EXPOSED_EDGE_AUTH_INCOMPLETE" /tmp/ff.log | sort -u | sed 's/^/   /'

hr; echo "### 2. Host-guard, DOC-AS-ORIGINALLY-WRITTEN (no RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS):"
mkdir -p /tmp/none; if run_server "RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/none"; then
  echo "   Host=api.khelsutra.guru -> $(code api.khelsutra.guru)   (expect 400 = the CUJ-killer)"
  echo "   Host=127.0.0.1          -> $(code 127.0.0.1)            (expect 200)"; stop_server
else echo "   FAILED to start (rc=$?)"; tail -4 /tmp/eng.log; fi

hr; echo "### 3. Host-guard FIX (RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS set):"
if run_server "RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/none RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru"; then
  echo "   Host=api.khelsutra.guru -> $(code api.khelsutra.guru)   (expect 200 = FIXED)"; stop_server
else echo "   FAILED to start"; tail -4 /tmp/eng.log; fi

hr; echo "### 4. REAL exposed config shape (edge_auth=true + dummy aud + RALLY_BIND_HOST=lo + allowlist) boots loopback-bound:"
mkdir -p /tmp/cfg_full; printf '%s' '{"security":{"edge_auth_enabled":true,"allowed_video_root":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming","upload_dir":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads","cf_team_domain":"https://khelsutra.cloudflareaccess.com","cf_access_aud":"DUMMY-FOR-BOOT-TEST"}}' > /tmp/cfg_full/config.json
if run_server "RALLY_APP_HOME=/tmp/cfg_full RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1 RALLY_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1 RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS=https://app.khelsutra.guru"; then
  echo "   bind: $(ss -tlnp 2>/dev/null | grep ':8000' | awk '{print $4}' | head -1)   (expect 127.0.0.1:8000, NOT 0.0.0.0)"

    keep the prior good results intact — a failed/empty re-run never destroys prior data.

A single, discoverable entry point so you (or a reviewer / CI) can verify the
whole suite before opening a PR:

    python run_all_tests.py             # full suite, quiet
    python run_all_tests.py -v          # verbose (passthrough to pytest)
    python run_all_tests.py -k config   # filter by keyword (passthrough)
    python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov-report=term-missing   # coverage

Anything after the script name is passed straight through to pytest.

Notes
-----
- The suite is fully mocked (no GPU, no network, no WSL) and runs offline.
- The observability **reporting** tests skip cleanly if the optional ``obsreport``
  package isn't installed (they use ``pytest.importorskip``). To exercise them too:
      pip install -e ../sports-obsreport --no-build-isolation
- Exit code is pytest's own, so this is CI / pre-PR friendly (0 = all green).
"""

@router.put("/api/upload/{upload_id}/part/{index}")
async def upload_part(upload_id: str, index: int, request: Request):
    """Append one part (raw bytes body). Parts are strictly sequential (0,1,2,...)."""
    _, max_bytes, part_bytes, _ = _upload_cfg()
    with _uploads_lock:
        st = _uploads.get(upload_id)
    if st is None:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Unknown upload_id '{upload_id}'")
    if index != st["parts"]:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=409,
                            detail=f"Out-of-order part: expected {st['parts']}, got {index} (parts are sequential)")

    received = 0

class UploadInitRequest(BaseModel):
    filename: str
    total_size_bytes: Optional[int] = None


class UploadCompleteRequest(BaseModel):
    total_parts: int


def _upload_cfg():
    # Resolved lazily (import inside the function) to read the LIVE startup config that
    # main() sets on the module global, and to avoid a circular import at module load
    # (main.py imports this router; this module must not import main at import time).
    import backend.main as _main
    sec = getattr(_main.global_config, "security", None)
    upload_dir = getattr(sec, "upload_dir", None) or "output/uploads"
    max_bytes = int(getattr(sec, "max_upload_mb", 4096) or 4096) * 1024 * 1024
    part_bytes = int(getattr(sec, "max_part_mb", 95) or 95) * 1024 * 1024
    exts = [e.lower().lstrip(".") for e in (getattr(sec, "allowed_upload_extensions", None) or ["mp4", "mov"])]
    return upload_dir, max_bytes, part_bytes, exts


def _abort_upload(upload_id: str) -> None:
    with _uploads_lock:
        st = _uploads.pop(upload_id, None)
    if st:
        try:
            os.remove(st["tmp_path"])
        except OSError:
            pass


@router.post("/api/upload/init")
def upload_init(req: UploadInitRequest):
    """Start a chunked upload. Returns upload_id; PUT sequential parts next."""
    upload_dir, max_bytes, part_bytes, exts = _upload_cfg()
    try:
        name = safe_output_filename(req.filename)
    except ValueError as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e
    ext = os.path.splitext(name)[1].lower().lstrip(".")
    if ext not in exts:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400,
                            detail=f"Extension '.{ext}' not allowed. Allowed: {sorted(exts)}")
    if req.total_size_bytes is not None and req.total_size_bytes > max_bytes:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=413,
                            detail=f"File exceeds the {max_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB upload limit.")
    os.makedirs(upload_dir, exist_ok=True)
    upload_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
    tmp_path = os.path.join(upload_dir, f".partial-{upload_id}")
    open(tmp_path, "wb").close()
    with _uploads_lock:
        _uploads[upload_id] = {"filename": name, "tmp_path": tmp_path, "parts": 0, "bytes": 0}
    return {"upload_id": upload_id, "max_part_mb": part_bytes // (1024 * 1024),
            "next_part": f"/api/upload/{upload_id}/part/0"}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

@app.post("/api/process", status_code=202)
def process_video(req: ProcessRequest, request: Request):
    """Submit a processing job (202 + job_id). Poll GET /api/jobs/{job_id} for state.

    Fast-fail checks (bad path, missing file, unknown segmenter) happen here so the
    client gets an immediate 4xx; everything slow — including MD5 hashing of the file —
    runs on the worker (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16). The row is created 'queued'; the
    worker drains it via `claim_due_job` (EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3 self-scheduling loop).
    """
    # M9 (D9): edge-auth tenant resolution. DEFAULT-OFF → owner_id None (the local single-user,
    # unchanged). When edge_auth_enabled, the Cf-Access JWT is required + verified (a missing or
    # spoofed identity header fails here with 401, before any work) and tags the job's owner_id.
    try:
        owner_id = edge_auth.resolve_tenant(request.headers, global_config)
    except edge_auth.EdgeAuthError as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=f"edge auth: {e}") from e

    allowed_root = getattr(getattr(global_config, "security", None), "allowed_video_root", None)
    try:
        validate_input_path(req.video_path, allowed_root=allowed_root)
        # M2: resolve the input once (path or storage URI) — raises ValueError for an unsupported
        # scheme, which we map to a clean 400 (an unknown scheme must never 500).
        input_ref = storage.resolve_input_ref(req.video_path, getattr(global_config, "storage", None))
    except ValueError as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e
    # The local-existence fast-fail applies only to a LOCAL input, and stats the RESOLVED local path
    # (input_ref.key — e.g. local://… stripped to the bare path), not the raw URI string. A
    # bucket/Drive URI can't be cheaply os.path.exists()'d — its existence is verified when the worker
    # fetches it (a fetch miss fails the job loudly). validate_input_path above still runs for every
    # input: its null-byte guard always applies, and a configured allowed_video_root (hosted mode)
    # rejects a non-local URI as out-of-root.
    if input_ref.backend == "local" and not os.path.exists(input_ref.key):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Video file not found at '{req.video_path}'")
    if req.segmenter and not segmenter_registry.get(req.segmenter):
        available = list(segmenter_registry.list_available().keys())
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=400,
            detail=f"Segmenter '{req.segmenter}' not found. Available segmenters: {available}"
        )

    job_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
    if not db_instance.create_job(job_id, req.video_path, req.segmenter,
                                  req.player_pool, req.max_frames, owner_id=owner_id,
                                  locale=req.locale):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Failed to persist job record.")
    _get_executor().submit(_drain_due_jobs)
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=202,
        content={"job_id": job_id, "status": "queued", "poll": f"/api/jobs/{job_id}"},
    )


@app.get("/api/jobs/{job_id}/cost")
[/external_agent_tool_result]

  -d '{"config":{"ingress":[{"hostname":"api.khelsutra.guru","service":"http://127.0.0.1:8000"},{"service":"http_status:404"}]}}' \

"

echo "=== DNS: api.khelsutra.guru CNAME -> ${TID}.cfargotunnel.com (proxied) ==="
curl -s -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d "{\"type\":\"CNAME\",\"name\":\"api.khelsutra.guru\",\"content\":\"${TID}.cfargotunnel.com\",\"proxied\":true}" \
  "https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/zones/$ZONE/dns_records" | python -c "
import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin)
print('  dns:',d.get('success'),'| errors:',d.get('errors'))
r=d.get('result') or {};print('  record:',r.get('name'),'->',r.get('content'),'proxied',r.get('proxied'))
"
echo "TID=$TID" > /tmp/khel_tid.txt; echo "(tunnel id saved locally for the next step)"
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## 🎉 The alpha is live end-to-end except DNS + the key

**Deployed & verified:**
- **Engine** — `origin/master` on the droplet, `systemd khelsutra-engine` **active**, bound `127.0.0.1:8000`, `edge_auth` ON, AUD wired, **`indexing.default_segmenter=gemini`**. Site (`:8787`) untouched.
- **Tunnel** — `khelsutra-studio` connected to CF (Mumbai/Bangalore edges), ingress `api.→127.0.0.1:8000`.
- **Access** — self-hosted app over both hostnames, one-time-PIN policy allowing `avi.dullu@gmail.com`, CORS/OPTIONS configured.
- **SPA** — built with `VITE_API_BASE=https://api.khelsutra.guru/api`, **live at https://khelsutra.pages.dev**; `app.khelsutra.guru` custom domain added (pending DNS).

**Exhaustive test results on the box:**

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| Full suite (`run_all_tests.py`) | ✅ **1313 passed**, 12 skipped |
| ruff / mypy | ✅ clean / clean (133 files) |
| Boot-posture fail-fast · Host-guard 400→200 · loopback bind | ✅ |
| Path-jail: `gs://`/`s3://`/`gdrive://`/`/etc/passwd`/`..` | ✅ all 400 |
| T4 upload → lands in jail · in-process jobs (no worker svc) | ✅ |
| Edge-auth gate: 401 no/garbage JWT, JWKS reachable | ✅ |

**Two findings (your "worth the money"):**
- **A — fixed:** the SPA sends *no* segmenter, so the engine's default (`yolo_hybrid`, needs torch) would've failed on this CPU box with `No module named 'torch'`. I set `indexing.default_segmenter=gemini` (torch-free) in the deployed config. *(I'll also fold this into the committed kit so it's not lost.)*
- **B — to route to the serving session:** with no key, the gemini segmenter logs the missing-key error but the job still finishes **`done` with 0 rallies** (error only in the server log, not the job status). Bad alpha UX — the engine should surface a missing key as a job failure. Not a deploy blocker (just set the key).

The alpha was **deployed and exhaustively tested on the CPU droplet** (`168.144.126.176`) this session. Folding the findings back into the kit so it matches tested reality.

### The load-bearing fix — `indexing.default_segmenter=gemini`
The SPA's `processVideo()` sends **no segmenter**, so the engine uses its config default. The shipped default is **`yolo_hybrid`** (needs torch/GPU) → on a CPU box every job fails `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'torch'` (reproduced live). The torch-free **`gemini`** segmenter is the CPU path. Added to `DEPLOY_ALPHA.md §2.2` + `droplet/config.json.example` (JSON-validated).

- `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` now includes `localhost,127.0.0.1` (for on-box health checks alongside the tunnel host).

Documented the **no-key silent-0-rallies** gotcha.

Doc/example only — no app code.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2

=== QueryRequest ===
class QueryRequest(BaseModel):
    video_id: str
    server: Optional[str] = None
    receiver: Optional[str] = None
    duration_min: Optional[float] = None
    event_type: Optional[str] = None

class CompileRequest(BaseModel):
=== /api/query handler ===
@app.post("/api/query")
def query_play_segments(req: QueryRequest):
    filters = {
        "server": req.server,
        "receiver": req.receiver,
        "duration_min": req.duration_min,
        "event_type": req.event_type
    }
    segments = db_instance.query_play_segments(req.video_id, filters)
    return {"play_segments": segments}

@app.post("/api/compile")
=== /api/jobs handler shape ===
@app.get("/api/jobs/{job_id}")
def get_job(job_id: str):
    """Job state: {status, progress, result, reports}. `status` = execution state
    (queued|running|done|failed); the pipeline verdict is result["status"]."""
    job = db_instance.get_job(job_id)
    if not job:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Unknown job_id '{job_id}'")
    result = job.get("result")
    return {
        "job_id": job["id"],
        "status": job["status"],
        "progress": job.get("progress"),
        "video_id": job.get("video_id"),
        "error": job.get("error"),
        "result": result,
        "reports": (result or {}).get("reports"),
        "created_at": job.get("created_at"),
        "started_at": job.get("started_at"),
        "finished_at": job.get("finished_at"),
    }

# --- Chunked upload (B2, PR-2) -------------------------------------------------------
# The chunked-upload island (in-process registry + helpers + the 4 endpoints) now lives in
# backend.api.uploads as a self-contained APIRouter. It resolves the live startup config via
# backend.main.global_config, so behavior — route paths, status codes, body-size limits,
# sequential-part logic, and abort cleanup — is unchanged.
from backend.api import uploads as uploads_api

app.include_router(uploads_api.router)


# --- Highlight download (B3, PR-2) ----------------------------------------------------

@app.get("/api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}")

### 369. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.858Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Full real-Gemini CUJ on isolated :8001 instance
command: ssh -i ~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet -o BatchMode=yes root@168.144.126.176 'bash -s' <<'EOF' 2>&1 | tail -40
set +e
PY=/opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/python; R=/opt/khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer
mkdir -p /srv/khelsutra/cujtest
cat > /srv/khelsutra/cujtest/config.json <<'JSON'
{"security":{"edge_auth_enabled":false,"allowed_video_root":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming","upload_dir":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads"},"indexing":{"default_segmenter":"gemini"}}
JSON
grep '^GEMINI_API_KEY=' /etc/khelsutra/engine.env > /srv/khelsutra/cujtest/.env
chown -R khelsutra:khelsutra /srv/khelsutra/cujtest; chmod 600 /srv/khelsutra/cujtest/.env
cd "$R"
sudo -u khelsutra env RALLY_APP_HOME=/srv/khelsutra/cujtest $PY -m backend.main --server --port 8001 >/tmp/cuj.log 2>&1 &
SV=$!
for i in $(seq 1 50); do curl -s -o /dev/null --max-time 1 http://127.0.0.1:8001/api/health 2>/dev/null && break; kill -0 $SV 2>/dev/null || { echo "ENGINE DIED:"; tail -12 /tmp/cuj.log; exit 1; }; sleep 0.5; done
B=http://127.0.0.1:8001
echo "### PROCESS sample.mp4 via REAL Gemini (locale=kn) ###"
JOB=$(curl -s -X POST -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"video_path":"/srv/khelsutra/incoming/uploads/sample.mp4","locale":"kn"}' $B/api/process | $PY -c "import sys,json;print(json.load(sys.stdin).get('job_id',''))")
echo "  job_id: $JOB"; VID=""
for i in $(seq 1 60); do
  J=$(curl -s $B/api/jobs/$JOB); ST=$(echo "$J" | $PY -c "import sys,json;print(json.load(sys.stdin).get('status','?'))")
  echo "  [$i] $ST"
  case "$ST" in done|completed|failed|error)
    VID=$(echo "$J" | $PY -c "import sys,json;print(json.load(sys.stdin).get('video_id') or '')")
    echo "  verdict: $(echo "$J" | $PY -c "import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin);r=d.get('result') or {};print('pipeline',r.get('status'),'| segment_count',r.get('segment_count'),'| err',str(d.get('error') or '')[:120])")"; break;; esac
  sleep 3
done
echo "### QUERY rallies (video_id=$VID) ###"
SEG=$(curl -s -X POST -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d "{\"video_id\":\"$VID\"}" $B/api/query)
echo "  $(echo "$SEG" | $PY -c "import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin);s=d.get('play_segments') or [];print('play_segments:',len(s));print('  first keys:',list(s[0].keys()) if s else 'none')")"
IDS=$(echo "$SEG" | $PY -c "import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin);s=d.get('play_segments') or [];print(','.join(str(x.get('id') if x.get('id') is not None else x.get('segment_id')) for x in s if (x.get('id') is not None or x.get('segment_id') is not None)))")
if [ -n "$IDS" ]; then
  echo "### COMPILE kn-localized reel (segment_ids=[$IDS]) ###"
  CMP=$(curl -s -X POST -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d "{\"video_id\":\"$VID\",\"segment_ids\":[$IDS],\"output_filename\":\"reel_kn.mp4\",\"locale\":\"kn\"}" $B/api/compile)
  echo "  compile resp: $(echo "$CMP" | head -c 220)"
  FN=$(echo "$CMP" | $PY -c "import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin);print(d.get('filename') or d.get('output_filename') or 'reel_kn.mp4')")
  curl -s -o /tmp/reel_kn.mp4 "$B/api/highlights/$VID/$FN"
  echo "### VERIFY downloaded reel ###"
  echo "  bytes: $(stat -c%s /tmp/reel_kn.mp4 2>/dev/null)"
  ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration:stream=codec_name,width,height -of default=nw=1 /tmp/reel_kn.mp4 2>&1 | sed 's/^/  /'
else
  echo "  -> 0 rallies in the 12s clip (Gemini + pipeline RAN — key & path proven; clip just too short for a rally)"
  echo "  gemini log tail: $(grep -iE 'gemini|rally|segment|candidate|chunk' /tmp/cuj.log | tail -3)"
fi
kill $SV 2>/dev/null; wait $SV 2>/dev/null
rm -rf /srv/khelsutra/cujtest /tmp/reel_kn.mp4
echo "### test instance stopped; production :8000 = $(systemctl is-active khelsutra-engine) ###"
EOF

- **SPA** — built with the split API base, **live on Pages** (`app.khelsutra.guru`).

### Exhaustive test results

| Area | Result |
|---|---|
| Full suite / ruff / mypy (on the box) | ✅ **1313 passed** · clean · clean |

### Two findings worth keeping
- **A (fixed + merged in [#85](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/85)):** the SPA sends no segmenter, so the engine used its default `yolo_hybrid` (needs torch) → failed on CPU. Set `indexing.default_segmenter=gemini` in the live config + the kit.
- **B (route to the serving session):** with an *invalid/absent* key, a job finishes `done` with 0 rallies and the error only hits the log, not the job status. Not active now (your key works), but the engine should surface a key failure as a job failure.

### What's left — only what needs you

## "Can I download a package and run it on a new machine now?" — Yes, for a developer. Not yet one-click.

**Works today** (verified on engine `origin/master`): `pip install` the wheel → `indexer --app` → it boots a localhost server, auto-picks a free port, and opens the **bundled SPA in your browser**. LIGHT tier (Gemini + your key, **torch-free**). That's genuinely "download + run" — *if* you're comfortable running one terminal command.

**Not there yet** for a *non-technical* user on a fresh box — three concrete gaps:
1. **No install path** beyond `pip`/`uv` — no install script, no default config.json, no uninstaller (**P2b-install**, un-shipped).
2. **No first-run wizard** — `/api/capabilities` exists on the engine but nothing in the SPA consumes it, so they hit a raw UI with no guided way to paste their Gemini key or pick compute (**P2c**, un-shipped).
3. **ffmpeg is still a manual dependency** (the resolver finds it but doesn't install it).

→ **Two work items (P2b-install + P2c wizard) stand between "a dev can run it" and "a co-founder can download and run it."** Both are Claude-doable.

59	def _job_to_request(job: Dict[str, Any]):
60	    """Reconstruct the submit-time ProcessRequest from a claimed job row, so a job
61	    re-claimed by `claim_due_job` (queued OR resumed-from-waiting) runs identically to
62	    the original submit. The row stores exactly the ProcessRequest fields."""

71	
72	def _run_claimed_job(job: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
73	    """Run ONE job already claimed (`status='running'`) by `claim_due_job`, recording its
74	    terminal state — OR parking it for resume on `JobWaiting` (WAIT_AND_RESUME).
75	
76	    Job `status` is the EXECUTION state (queued|waiting|running|done|failed): 'done' means
77	    the worker finished — the pipeline's own verdict (validation failure etc.) lives in
78	    result["status"]; 'failed' means the worker itself crashed/raised; 'waiting' means the
79	    job self-scheduled and will be re-claimed when `next_poll_at` is due.
80	    """
81	    import backend.main as _main
82	    db_instance = _main.db_instance
83	    job_id = job["id"]
84	    req = _main._job_to_request(job)
85	    try:
86	        result = _main._execute_processing(
87	            req, progress=lambda stage: db_instance.set_job_progress(job_id, stage))
88	        if result.get("video_id"):
89	            db_instance.set_job_video_id(job_id, result["video_id"])
90	        db_instance.mark_job_done(job_id, result)
91	        _maybe_record_job_cost(job, result)  # M8 cost ledger (default-OFF)
92	    except _main.JobWaiting as w:
93	        # Cooperative re-enqueue: persist progress + next_poll_at, release the worker.
94	        logger.info(f"Job {job_id} parked for {w.delay_sec:.0f}s (resume when due).")
95	        db_instance.set_job_waiting(job_id, w.delay_sec, progress=w.progress)
96	    except Exception as e:
97	        logger.error(f"Job {job_id} crashed: {e}\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
98	        db_instance.mark_job_failed(job_id, f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}")

104	    (= {gpu_seconds, wall_seconds, egress_gb, gemini_usd, gpu_type}) × the versioned pricebook, and

description: Push + open Finding B PR
command: WT=/c/Users/avidu/Projects/_bhi_work
git -C "$WT" push -u origin fix/finding-b-loud-unrunnable-config 2>&1 | tail -3
gh pr create --repo Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer --base master --head fix/finding-b-loud-unrunnable-config \
  --title "fix(segmenter): fail the job on an unrunnable config instead of a silent 0-rally success (Finding B)" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Why
Surfaced while deploying the alpha on the CPU droplet. With **no `GEMINI_API_KEY`**, `GeminiPlaySegmenter` logged an error but returned `[]` → `_execute_processing` returned a **`success` with 0 segments** → the job finished `done` and the SPA rendered an **empty reel as if it worked**. A non-technical tester who forgot the key gets a fake "done"; the operator only sees the log. That violates the loud-failures bar.

## Fix (uses the contract that already exists)
- `_resolve_provider` returns a **`Failure`** (with a user-facing reason) instead of `None` on the three **unrunnable** cases: missing API key, unknown sport, unknown provider. `process_video` propagates it.
- A **genuine** "ran, found nothing" (key present, provider returns 0) is **unchanged** — still an empty-list success.

This needed no new plumbing: `_execute_processing` already maps a `Failure` → `result.status="failed"` + message (main.py:521), the SPA already treats a `done` job with `result.status === "failed"` as a failure (`useIngestJob.ts:88-95`), and the CLI worker already returns rc 3 on a `Failure`. The segmenter just wasn't using it.

## Tests
+3 in `test_gemini.py`: missing-key → `Failure` (asserts the reason), unknown-sport → `Failure`, and **genuine-empty stays a non-`Failure` success** (the important counterpart). Full suite **1335 passed / 7 skipped**; ruff + mypy clean.

## Scope
Engine-only, no API/SPA change. Default behavior for a correctly-configured engine is identical. Built in an isolated `origin/master` worktree (serving session's checkout untouched).
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 483. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.864Z)

All clean. Here's what's waiting for you. ☕

## 🎁 Shipped while you showered — Finding B, live and verified

The one gap that could quietly burn a real alpha tester is closed. Before: a missing/invalid Gemini key made a job finish **"done" with an empty reel** — a fake success. Now it **fails loudly** with the reason.

- **Engine fix** ([#311](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/311), merged): the gemini segmenter returns a `Failure` (with a user-facing message) on the three *unrunnable* cases — missing key, unknown sport, unknown provider — instead of a silent empty. A genuine "ran, found nothing" is untouched.
- **No new plumbing** — it rides contracts that already existed: the API maps `Failure → result.status="failed"`, and the SPA already shows a failed-result job as a friendly error (so the tester sees "that didn't work," not a fake reel; the operator sees the exact reason in the log + API).
- **Verified the whole way:** +3 tests, full suite **1335 passed**, ruff + mypy clean, **all 9 CI lanes green**, and **redeployed + proven on the live box** — a keyless job now returns `result.status=failed, message="GEMINI_API_KEY is not set…"`. Your live `:8000` engine (with your real key) is healthy and unchanged for normal use.

## 🧹 Clean slate
Both repos on their branches, **no worktrees, no running test VMs/instances**, the serving session's checkout untouched, memory + handoff updated. Bonus: I checked the flagged `cloudflared cert.pem` worry — **it's a non-issue** (the API-token tunnel I set up leaves no account-wide cert on the box).

## Why I *didn't* rush P2b (the "download & run" packaging step)
It's the clear #2, but it hinges on **one decision that's genuinely yours**, not mine: the repo is **private**, so for a non-technical user to "download a package," you first have to pick the channel —

- **Publish to PyPI / make it public** → they run `uv tool install khelsutra-indexer` (cleanest, but it's a go-public call), or
- **Ship a downloadable `.whl`** they install from a file (keeps the repo private).

Once you pick, the rest of P2b (install/uninstall scripts + a truly-local default config + the ffmpeg check) is a quick, well-scoped build — and its real done-gate is a *clean Windows VM → installer → reel*, which needs a fresh machine I can't spin up solo. So I left it crisply scoped rather than ship a half-verified installer (the exact thing we agreed to never do).

**When you're back and toweled off:** the most satisfying thing to do is **open `app.khelsutra.guru`, sign in with the email OTP, and upload a real match** — that's the one step only you can drive, and I'd love to hear how the first real reel turns out. And whenever you want P2b, just tell me **PyPI-public or downloadable-wheel** and I'll build it. 🏸

### 494. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.864Z)

**Root cause confirmed.** The SPA's `fetch` (client.ts:60-64) sends **no `credentials: 'include'`** — so its cross-origin calls to `api.khelsutra.guru` never carry the Cloudflare Access cookie. Access bounces every API call to the login page → the fetch fails → "Can't reach KhelSutra." This is the classic **split-origin + Access cross-cookie gotcha** (the `app.` and `api.` subdomains are different origins, and the Access session can't ride along). It's not the file size — it would fail on *any* upload.

The robust fix is **single-origin**: the engine already bundles the SPA (P1b), so I'll serve the SPA *and* the API from **one** hostname behind **one** Access cookie. Let me check the engine's SPA-serving + bundle mechanics on the box to do this cleanly:

# P1b: serve the bundled KhelSutra SPA single-origin. `backend/ui/` is the committed snapshot
# (produced by scripts/bundle_ui.sh); fall back to the legacy in-engine dashboard only if it's
# absent (a source checkout with no vendored UI). The SPA calls same-origin `/api`, so no SPA change
# is needed; it is query-param driven (?video=…), so serving index.html at `/` + /assets is enough
# (no client-side path routing → no history-fallback catch-all, which would also be a route-order hazard).
_BUNDLED_UI_DIR = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "ui")
_UI_DIR = _BUNDLED_UI_DIR if os.path.isfile(os.path.join(_BUNDLED_UI_DIR, "index.html")) else _STATIC_DIR
_UI_ASSETS_DIR = os.path.join(_UI_DIR, "assets")
if os.path.isdir(_UI_ASSETS_DIR):
    app.mount("/assets", StaticFiles(directory=_UI_ASSETS_DIR), name="assets")


@app.get("/", response_class=HTMLResponse)
def serve_index():
    index_path = os.path.join(_UI_DIR, "index.html")
    if os.path.exists(index_path):
        with open(index_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            return f.read()
    return "<h3>Sports Highlight Indexer Server is Running. (No bundled UI found.)</h3>"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Bundle the KhelSutra web SPA into the engine so a downloaded engine serves the real UI
# single-origin (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P1b).
#
# OPTION 1 (now, owner-chosen): build the SPA from a khelsutra checkout and vendor the built `dist/`
# into `backend/ui/`, which is COMMITTED — so the wheel + CI build fully offline/reproducibly. Refresh
# the snapshot by re-running this on an SPA release; commit the diff.
#
# OPTION 3 (designed-for, future): point --from at a published dist tarball/URL (a GitHub Release
# artifact) to vendor a pre-built SPA without a local khelsutra checkout or Node. Already wired below.
#
# Usage:
#   scripts/bundle_ui.sh [SOURCE]
#     SOURCE = a khelsutra checkout dir (default: $KHELSUTRA_DIR or ../khelsutra)   [builds it]
#            | a path/URL ending .tgz/.tar.gz                                       [unpacks it]
#   BUILD=0 scripts/bundle_ui.sh <khelsutra-dir>   # skip npm; reuse an existing web/dist
set -euo pipefail

ENGINE_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
UI_DEST="$ENGINE_ROOT/backend/ui"
SOURCE="${1:-${KHELSUTRA_DIR:-$ENGINE_ROOT/../khelsutra}}"

stage_dist() {  # populates $1 (a dist dir) into a clean $UI_DEST
  local dist="$1"
  [ -f "$dist/index.html" ] || { echo "ERROR: no index.html in $dist — not a built SPA dist" >&2; exit 1; }

  -d "{\"type\":\"CNAME\",\"name\":\"app.khelsutra.guru\",\"content\":\"${TID}.cfargotunnel.com\",\"proxied\":true}" \
  "$CF/zones/$ZONE/dns_records/$RID" | python -c "import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin);r=d.get('result') or {};print('  app. ->',r.get('content'),'| success:',d.get('success'))"
echo "=== 3. release app. from Pages (so DNS routes to the tunnel cleanly) ==="
curl -s -X DELETE -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "$CF/accounts/$ACC/pages/projects/khelsutra/domains/app.khelsutra.guru" | python -c "import sys,json;d=json.load(sys.stdin);print('  pages domain removed:',d.get('success'),d.get('errors') or '')"

### 509. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.865Z)

**Solved — and your engine was running the whole time.** The "can't reach" was neither the engine being down nor the file size; I verified a real **95 MiB chunk uploads in ~2 seconds** (HTTP 200). The actual culprit:

## Root cause: the split-origin Cloudflare-Access cookie
You were on `app.khelsutra.guru` (served by Pages) but the API lives on `api.khelsutra.guru` (the tunnel) — **different origins**. The SPA's `fetch` doesn't send credentials, so its calls to `api.` never carried your Access session cookie → Access bounced every API call to the login page → the browser reports it as "can't reach." It would've failed on *any* upload, not just 500 MB.

## The fix: single-origin — and it's working now
The engine already bundles the SPA, so I pointed everything at **one origin behind one Access cookie**. **`https://api.khelsutra.guru` now serves the whole app** (SPA at `/`, API at `/api`). Verified just now via the real path:
- `GET /` → **200**, `<title>KhelSutra Studio</title>` (SPA loads)
- `/api/health` → 200, `upload/init` → 200, **95 MiB + 50 MiB chunk PUTs → 200**

**👉 Use `https://api.khelsutra.guru` and the upload will work.** (Yes, the `api.` URL is a bit ugly — see below to get `app.` back.)

## Two follow-ups
1. **To restore `app.khelsutra.guru`** (nicer URL): it needs its DNS record repointed to the tunnel, and **I couldn't make that edit — the `Zone.DNS:Edit` scope you added didn't take on this token** (DNS writes still return "auth error"). Easiest path: in the Cloudflare dashboard → `khelsutra.guru` → DNS, add/replace:
   - **CNAME** `app` → `55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`, **Proxied (orange cloud)**
   
   Then `app.khelsutra.guru` works single-origin too. (Or just keep using `api.` — same app.)
2. **The engine serves a slightly older SPA snapshot** (commit #68 — full upload→reel flow + all 6 languages, but built before the *localized reel-watermark* wiring). Great for testing now; I can refresh it to the latest so kn/hi users get their reel watermarked in-language. Want me to?

Go try an upload on **`api.khelsutra.guru`** — fingers crossed for your first real reel. 🏸 And tell me if you want me to push the latest SPA + (once your DNS edit lands or the token's fixed) flip it back to `app.`.

### 511. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.866Z)

Three things — including a heads-up that'll save you the next failure.

## ⚠️ Before you retry: `gs://` won't work on the hosted box
This is the *other* deploy caveat (the path-jail). On the exposed engine, `allowed_video_root` is required, and `/api/process` validates the path is local-under-jail **before** resolving the scheme — so a `gs://…` URI is rejected with a 400. **Browser upload is the only ingest path on the hosted box right now.** (I'm filing this as an issue too — making `gs://` work there is a real, wanted feature, just a careful engine change.) So please retry the upload on **`api.khelsutra.guru`**, not a bucket URI.

## To be clear: there's no size limit to increase
Nothing to tune there — I verified a **95 MiB chunk uploads in ~2 seconds** (and 50 MiB, both HTTP 200). The "can't reach" was **100% the cross-origin auth cookie**, now fixed by single-origin. So: don't worry about a size knob.

## Your worry about the screencasts — here's the honest read
**The product is fine; the screencasts tested a valid path.** They exercised the *local/direct* CUJ (SPA → engine on localhost / the bucket flow) — and that genuinely works; I re-verified the whole upload→reel chain again today on the single-origin path. What broke was **only the new hosted deploy topology** (split-origin + Cloudflare Access), a layer the screencasts never touched.

And I'll own the real gap squarely: I verified *every* layer — DNS, tunnel, the Access gate, the engine, even a **full-chain test through Access** — but that test used a **service token (header auth)**, while a browser uses **cookie auth**. The cookie path is exactly what cross-origin breaks, so my test passed green while the browser failed. **The one layer that needed a real browser is the one that broke** — and that's precisely what your "basic test" caught. That's not the product failing; that's alpha testing doing its job, on day one, with the only user being you. Good catch, genuinely.

## Debugging / tracing — what we have, and the gap
- **For *this* class of failure, engine logs won't help** — Access blocked the requests at Cloudflare's edge, before they ever reached the engine. The right place is **Cloudflare → Zero Trust → Logs → Access** (every allow/block/login at the edge).
- **For requests that reach the engine:** `journalctl -u khelsutra-engine` on the droplet logs every request; the SPA threads an `x-correlation-id` you can match between the browser console and the engine log; per-video `obsreport` captures each processing run.
- **The gap you're sensing is real:** there's no unified request **capture/replay** store. I'm filing that.

Filing now:

  --title "Hosted alpha: split-origin (Pages app. + tunnel api.) breaks Cloudflare Access for the SPA → single-origin needed" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Symptom
On the hosted alpha, the SPA loaded at `app.khelsutra.guru` but **every upload failed "Can't reach KhelSutra. Is it running?"** — even though the engine was healthy.

## Root cause (NOT file size)
`app.khelsutra.guru` (Cloudflare Pages) and `api.khelsutra.guru` (cloudflared tunnel) are **different origins**. The SPA's `fetch` (`web/src/api/client.ts:60`) sends **no `credentials: 'include'`**, so cross-origin calls to `api.` never carry the Cloudflare Access cookie → Access `302`s every API call to the login page → the fetch rejects → "can't reach." It fails on *any* upload, not just large ones.

**Verified it's not size:** a real **95 MiB chunk PUT through the tunnel returned 200 in ~2s** (and 50 MiB → 200).

## Fix applied (2026-06-22) — single-origin
The engine already bundles the SPA (P1b), so I repointed to **single-origin**: `api.khelsutra.guru` now serves the **SPA at `/`** and the **API at `/api`** behind **one** Access cookie. Verified end-to-end via a service token: `GET /` → 200 (`<title>KhelSutra Studio</title>`), `/api/health` → 200, `upload/init` + 95 MiB + 50 MiB chunk PUTs → 200.

## Remaining / to pick up
1. **`app.khelsutra.guru` DNS** needs repointing to the tunnel: `CNAME app → 55142398-732c-4803-8a9c-48766f1f5cac.cfargotunnel.com`, proxied. Blocked on the CF API token lacking `Zone.DNS:Edit` (the scope add didn't take); do via dashboard or fix the token. (The Pages custom domain for `app.` was removed.)
2. **Update `deploy/DEPLOY_ALPHA.md`** to recommend **single-origin** (engine-served SPA) as the default for the SPA+Access case, OR document the cross-origin requirements if split is desired: SPA `credentials:'include'` + engine CORS `allow_credentials=true` + a way to pre-warm the `api.` Access cookie (the hard part for fetch).
3. **Refresh the engine's bundled SPA** (currently commit #68) to latest, so the localized reel-watermark (LW2 #73) + recent polish (#82/#83/#86) ship in the hosted app.
4. **Lesson:** a service-token full-chain test passes (header auth) while a browser cookie path fails — the hosted CUJ must be verified with a **real browser session**.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -1

echo "=== Issue 2 (engine): observability / request tracing + replay ==="
gh issue create --repo Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer \
  --title "Hosted-app observability: request tracing + replay for debugging production CUJ failures" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Context
A hosted alpha upload failure was hard to debug because the failing requests **never reached the engine** — Cloudflare Access `302`'d them at the edge, so `journalctl` showed nothing. We want first-class observability so production CUJ failures are diagnosable (and replayable) without guesswork.

## What exists today
- `x-correlation-id` threaded SPA ↔ engine.
- Structured engine logs (`journalctl -u khelsutra-engine`) — every request method/path/status.
- Per-video `obsreport` (observability report next to each processed video).
- **Cloudflare Zero Trust → Logs → Access** — the edge allow/block/login log (where the cross-origin failure was actually visible).

## Gap
- No unified **request capture/replay** store for the app's API requests.
- No easy way to **import/replay** captured requests for repro or regression.
- Edge (Access) failures aren't correlated with engine logs.

## Proposed
1. Optional request-capture middleware → a debug store (method, path, status, cid, redacted headers, timing), **default-OFF**, flag-gated.
2. A **replay harness** that loads captured requests and re-issues them against a target engine (repro + CUJ regression).
3. A debugging **runbook**: Access logs + `journalctl` + correlation-id flow.
4. Basic live-droplet monitoring (uptime / job-failure / Gemini-cost), ties to the alpha-ops hygiene item.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -1

echo "=== Issue 3 (engine): gs:// scheme-exemption on a jailed box ==="
gh issue create --repo Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer \
  --title "Allow gs:// / s3:// / gdrive-api:// ingest on an exposed (jailed) box — scheme-exempt recognized storage backends from the path-jail" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Today
When `security.allowed_video_root` is set (required to expose), `/api/process` runs `validate_input_path` (local-under-jail) **before** resolving the storage scheme, so `gs://bucket/x.mp4` → `os.path.realpath` escapes the jail → **400**. Bucket ingest is therefore impossible on the exposed alpha box. Pinned by `tests/test_api_endpoints.py::test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri` (gs/s3/gdrive all 400).

## Want
The owner wants to ingest large videos via `gs://` on the hosted box (far more convenient than a 500 MB browser upload).

## Proposed
Make `validate_input_path` / the `/api/process` preamble **scheme-aware**: either (a) allow recognized storage schemes (`gs://`, `s3://`, `gdrive-api://`) *before* the local-jail check, or (b) apply the jail **only to local refs** (validate after `storage.resolve_input_ref`, jailing only `backend == "local"`). 

⚠ Must NOT reopen the arbitrary-local-file hole — the jail must still fully apply to **local** paths (traversal/symlink/absolute). Add tests for both the allow (bucket URI accepted) and the reject (local traversal still 400) paths. Pairs with the `gs://` ingest the alpha CUJ + DEPLOY_ALPHA §7 currently disclaim.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -1
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 549. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.868Z)

**26 rallies found, 10 of them >5s — and your reel is stitching those 10 right now!** 🔥 The semantic query box filtering to >5s is exactly the intended magic.

Now, your question — and I measured it rather than guessed. Here's where the ~8.5 min of processing actually went:

| Phase | Time | Bound by |
|---|---|---|
| Your upload (7 chunks) | ~30 s | **your upstream bandwidth** (not CPU) |
| Validation | ~1.5 min | **CPU** (frame decode) |
| **Chunk extraction (6×)** | **~5.5 min** | **CPU** (ffmpeg re-encode/downscale) ← the big one |
| Gemini upload + analysis | ~1.5 min | **network + Google** (analysis was only ~15 s/chunk, in parallel) |
| Stitching (now) | in progress | **CPU** (ffmpeg encode of the reel) |

**So yes — you're reading it right: it's overwhelmingly CPU-bound.** ~65% of the wait is one thing: **ffmpeg re-encoding the six chunks on 2 cores** (it downscales each 90 s clip before sending to Gemini). Validation and stitching are CPU too. The Gemini *analysis* is fast and fixed (Google's side — hardware won't change it), and your upload is bandwidth.

**What actually moves the needle:**
1. **A GPU box is the biggest lever — by far.** NVENC/NVDEC do the encode/decode in *seconds* instead of minutes, collapsing the 5.5-min extraction and the stitch. And a GPU **also unlocks the owned WASB detector** (higher-quality rally detection) — so it's a speed *and* quality double-win. This is exactly why the roadmap points at GPU serving.
2. **More/faster CPU cores** help proportionally (software ffmpeg scales with cores) — a clear improvement over this 2-core droplet, just not as dramatic as a GPU.
3. **Software levers, no new hardware:** the chunk *downscale-re-encode* is the hot path — using hardware-accel ffmpeg, a faster preset, or skipping the downscale (trade upload bandwidth for less encode) would cut it. The repo already flags "ffmpeg extraction is the bottleneck."

Want me to **file a perf issue** capturing this breakdown (with the levers: GPU/NVENC, parallel extraction, the downscale trade-off) so it's tracked? It's a real, well-scoped optimization — and the measured numbers above make a clean baseline. Meanwhile… go grab that reel the second it drops. 🏸

### 558. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.868Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: File async-compile + MD5-reuse issues
command: echo "=== Issue: async /api/compile (the 524 fix) ==="
gh issue create --repo Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer \
  --title "[reliability] /api/compile is synchronous → Cloudflare 524 on slow stitches; make it async (202 + poll) like /api/process" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Symptom (hosted alpha, first real reel)
"Build reel" of 10 rallies (1:17) returned a **Cloudflare 524 (origin timeout)** and the SPA showed **"Build failed"** — but the engine had **succeeded**: `Dynamic highlight reel saved: /srv/khelsutra/output/highlights.mp4 (178.6 MB)`, downloadable at `GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/highlights.mp4` → 200. The user saw a failure for a reel that actually built (and never got the download URL).

## Root cause
`POST /api/compile` is **synchronous** — it runs the full stitch (ffmpeg **trim + re-encode each clip** + concat + watermark) before returning. On the 2-core CPU droplet, trimming 10 clips took ~15–27 s **each** (~3 min total) → past Cloudflare's **~100 s** proxy/edge timeout → **524**, while the engine kept going and finished.

## Fix
Make `/api/compile` **asynchronous**, mirroring `/api/process`: return **202 + a compile job id**, run the stitch on the background executor, and have the SPA **poll** for the reel URL (or reuse the job/poll machinery). This decouples reel-build from the request timeout entirely.

## Notes
- Even with a GPU, a **long** reel could exceed 100 s — async is the correct fix **regardless of hardware** (GPU/NVENC speeds the stitch, but doesn't remove the timeout risk).
- Cloudflare free-plan edge timeout (~100 s) isn't easily raised; the cloudflared tunnel is subject to it too. Async is the path.
- Pairs with #328 (per-video runlog) and #98/#99 (processing-state UX).
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -1

echo "=== Issue: MD5-based segmentation reuse ==="
gh issue create --repo Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer \
  --title "[optimization] MD5 segmentation reuse — skip Gemini re-segmentation when the same video (video_id) is re-uploaded" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Ask (from the owner)
"If I upload the same video, I hope we do an MD5 checksum to reuse the Gemini segmentation."

## Today
`video_id = compute_video_id(file)` **is** the MD5 of the file content, and a video's rallies are stored in `play_segments` keyed by `video_id`. But `/api/process` currently **re-runs the full segmentation** (Gemini) on every submit — re-paying Gemini (paid) and re-waiting (~8 min on the CPU box) even for an already-segmented, byte-identical file. No reuse short-circuit found in `main.py` / `job_worker.py`.

## Proposed
On `/api/process`, if `video_id` already has stored segments **for the same segmenter + relevant config**, **reuse** them and skip Gemini (return immediately with the existing rallies). Add an explicit **`force_resegment`** flag to override. The cache key already exists (MD5 `video_id`); this is mostly a short-circuit + the match condition.

## Care
- Only reuse when the segmenter and segmentation-affecting config match (don't serve `gemini` segments for a `wasb_hybrid` request).
- Keep the existing "destroy-after-confirm" re-ingest semantics for an explicit re-segment.
- Big win: Gemini is paid + the CPU re-processing is the slow path; reuse makes re-opening a video instant and free.
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -1
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## Measured on the live alpha (2-core CPU droplet), a real 411s (6.9 min) 1080p match → 26 rallies

| Phase | Wall time | Bound by | Lever |
|------|-----------|----------|-------|
| Upload (7 chunks) | ~30 s | client upstream bandwidth | (network, not us) |
| Validation | ~87 s | **CPU** (frame decode/validators) | GPU/NVDEC, faster CPU |
| **Chunk extraction (6× ffmpeg re-encode + downscale)** | **~5.5 min** | **CPU** (software libx264) | **GPU/NVENC**, more cores, **skip the downscale**, parallelize |
| Gemini upload + analysis | ~1.5 min | network (upload) + Gemini API (~15s/chunk, parallel) | (fixed — Google's side) |
| Reel stitch (trim+re-encode 10 clips → concat → watermark) | ~3 min | **CPU** (per-clip re-encode) | **GPU/NVENC**; also async-compile (#329) so it doesn't 524 |

**~65% of the wait is one thing:** ffmpeg re-encoding on 2 cores (extraction + per-clip trim). The Gemini *analysis* is fast and external; the upload is client bandwidth.

## Recommended levers (in impact order)
1. **GPU box (L4) with NVENC/NVDEC** — collapses extraction + stitch from minutes to seconds; **also unlocks the owned WASB detector** (higher rally-detection quality) → speed **and** quality. (Per ADR-012/013, GCP L4 / Cloud Run GPU.)
2. **Software (no new HW):** skip/relax the `ai_scale_width` chunk downscale (trade upload bytes for far less encode); parallelize extraction across cores; faster encode preset.
3. **Async `/api/compile`** (#329) so a slow stitch returns 202 + poll instead of a Cloudflare 524.

Baseline numbers above are a clean before/after reference once a GPU/optimizations land.
EOF

Based on the search results, here are the pricing details for Google Cloud Run with NVIDIA L4 GPUs:

## Per-Second and Per-Hour Pricing

In Tier 1 regions, an NVIDIA L4 is around $0.0001867 per second without zonal redundancy, which is roughly $0.67 per hour.

With zonal redundancy, pricing is higher (about $0.0002909 per second).

## Billing Model

GPU usage is billed per second separately from CPU and memory, and there is no free tier for GPUs. Cloud Run charges you only for the resources you use, rounded up to the nearest 100 millisecond.

## Important Notes

Exact rates vary by region and configuration. For the most current and region-specific pricing, you should check Google Cloud's official pricing page or use their Pricing Calculator, as rates may vary by location and change over time.

### 572. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.869Z)

Pricing verified. Here's your GCP GPU recommendation with real numbers.

## TL;DR recommendation: **Cloud Run + NVIDIA L4 (scale-to-zero)** for the alpha

The whole bottleneck is **ffmpeg encode/decode on CPU** (extraction + stitch = ~65% of the wait). An **L4 GPU does that in hardware (NVENC/NVDEC)** — collapsing the ~8.5 min to roughly **2–3 min/match** — *and* it unlocks the **owned WASB detector** (better rally quality). Double win.

### Cost breakdown (verified June 2026 pricing)

| Option | GPU | Rate | **Per match (~3 min)** | Idle cost | Best for |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **⭐ Cloud Run + L4 (scale-to-zero)** | L4 | **~$0.67/hr**, billed per-second | **~$0.03 GPU + ~$0.05 Gemini ≈ $0.08** | **$0** | the alpha (low/spiky volume) |
| Compute Engine `g2-standard-4` (always-on) | L4 | $0.71/hr on-demand · $0.62 spot | same compute | **~$516/mo** (~$454 spot) | steady high volume (>~500/day) |
| Compute Engine `n1 + T4` (budget always-on) | T4 | ~$0.54/hr | ~$0.03 + Gemini | ~$390/mo | budget always-on, lower quality |
| **Today: CPU droplet** | — | ~$24/mo | ~8.5 min **+ the 524** | ~$24/mo | the current pain |

**Why Cloud Run wins for you now:** at alpha volume (tens–hundreds of matches/mo) it's **~$0.08/match and $0 when idle** — e.g. 100 matches ≈ **$8 + Gemini**. A dedicated L4 VM only pays off above **~500 matches/day** (break-even ~15k/mo). And it's **cap-friendly** — pennies per match vs $516/mo. GPU spot discounts are weak (~12%) and risk preemption, so they're not worth it here.

**The migration is already half-built:** the engine has the **M6 Cloud Run Job dispatch shim + the L4 worker image** (`deploy/cloudrun/`), and a **real L4 run was proven last session — 22 rallies for ~$0.21**. The missing piece is **M7** (the persistent Cloud Run *service* + a deploy report) — which the doc-scan already flagged as the keystone.

### 575. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.869Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: File the &debug= tracing feature issue
command: gh issue create --repo avidullu/khelsutra \
  --title "SPA: &debug=1 (trace panel) / &debug=2 (full runlog) URL params for debugging + tracing" \
  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Idea (owner)
A `&debug=` ladder on the SPA URL so debugging/tracing is well-covered:
- `?video=…&debug=1` → surface **tracing** (a debug panel)
- `?video=…&debug=2` → the **entire runlog file** for that video/job

## Ground-truth mapping (what already exists — so this is mostly a surfacing layer)
- The SPA is already `?video=…`-driven and threads an **`x-correlation-id`** on every call.
- **`GET /api/jobs/{job_id}`** already returns `status`, `progress`, `result`, and `reports` (the per-video obsreport paths).
- The engine emits a **per-video `obsreport` / `report.json`** next to each processed video.
- A dedicated per-video trace endpoint is proposed in #328.

## Proposed design
- **`&debug=1` → in-UI trace panel**: the job's phase timeline + timings + chunk/segment counts + the correlation id, pulled from `/api/jobs/{id}` (and the #328 trace endpoint). Light, low-risk.
- **`&debug=2` → fetch/download the full runlog**: the structured obsreport for the job (+ optionally the raw engine log lines for that `cid`).

## Security (important on the hosted / Cloudflare-Access deploy)
- **Scope strictly to the signed-in user's own** job/video — `?video=X&debug=2` must not dump someone else's run.
- **Redact** secrets + sensitive local paths before surfacing the runlog.
- Consider gating **`&debug=2`** (raw runlog) to the **operator/owner**; `&debug=1` (trace panel) is fine for any authed user on their own job.
